I mean, because my schedule rotates so much, my body is just kind of confused all the time. So even on my days off, I wouldn't really say I sleep normally. It's pretty much strictly evenings and overnights with this contractor. So, uh, I couldn't really tell you if it would get better. I just never get a stretch of normal daytime hours to find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you use marijuana?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't use marijuana at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed مؤخراً?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I work overnight shifts, and a lot of times when I get home, I'll just lie in bed... like, I'm completely tired but somehow wired at the same time. And even when I do manage to get a decent number of hours of sleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Did these sleep problems start after you began working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's been going on for about three years now. That's when the contractor changed our schedules. They put us on these rotating evening and overnight shifts, mostly covering the larger properties, and my sleep just hasn't been the same since.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not work overnight, do you sleep normally?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure anymore. I mean, because my schedule rotates so much, my body is just kind of confused all the time. So even on my days off, I wouldn't really say I sleep normally. It's still pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: When your work schedule switches back to daytime hours for a while, does your sleep get better?\n\nPatient: I don't really get daytime shifts... like, at all anymore. I don't use marijuana at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed مؤخراً?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. It's just, like I said, that heavy feeling is kind of sitting there while I'm doing them. But I wouldn't say I've lost interest.\n\nDoctor: When your sleep is especially bad, does that heavy, flat feeling usually get worse?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really paid attention to if a bad night makes it heavier... it's just kind of a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that heavy, flat feeling most days?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now... about three years, I'd say. And even when I do manage to get a decent number of hours of sleep, I wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: Did these sleep problems start after you began working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much. It's been going on for about three years now. That's when the contractor changed our schedules. They put us on these rotating evening and overnight shifts, mostly covering the larger properties, and my sleep just hasn't been the same since.\n\nDoctor: On days when you do not work overnight, do you sleep normally?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure anymore. I mean, because my schedule rotates so much, my body is just kind of confused all the time. So even on my days off, I wouldn't really say I sleep normally. It's still pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: When your work schedule switches back to daytime hours for a while, does your sleep get better?\n\nPatient: I don't really get daytime shifts... like, at all anymore. It's pretty much strictly evenings and overnights with this contractor. So, uh, I couldn't really tell you if it would get better. I just never get a stretch of normal daytime hours to find out.\n\nDoctor: Do you use marijuana?\n\nPatient: No, I don't. I don't use marijuana at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed مؤخراً?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. Um... most days, I just feel like there's this heavy, flat feeling following me around. It doesn't really go away. Even when I'm just pushing my cleaning cart down an empty hallway at work, or... or if I'm sitting with my family and just trying to act normal, it's always kind of there.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I still do the stuff I normally like to do... like watching a game or just hanging out. I still want to do those things, and I still get something out of them. It's just, like I said, that heavy feeling is kind of sitting there while I'm doing them. But I wouldn't say I've lost interest.\n\nDoctor: When your sleep is especially bad, does that heavy, flat feeling usually get worse?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed a connection, to be honest. It feels pretty much the same most days, whether I get a decent night's sleep or not. I haven't really paid attention to if a bad night makes it heavier... it's just kind of a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that heavy, flat feeling most days?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now... about three years, I'd say. Yeah, it started right around the time the contractor changed our schedules. They moved us to these rotating evening and overnight shifts at some of the larger properties. It's just been kind of hanging over me ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel hopeless. I mean... I don't feel like giving up or anything like that. I just keep going to work and doing what I need to do every day. It's just that constant heavy feeling I mentioned before, but I wouldn't really call it hopelessness.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel anxious or jittery or anything like that. It's really just that flat, heavy feeling I was talking about. I'm not on edge, just... tired and flat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you get home from an overnight shift and try to sleep, do you often have trouble actually falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty messed up for a long time now. When I get home from an overnight shift, I'll just lie there in bed... I'm exhausted, but it's like I'm tired but wired at the same time, so I can't actually drift off. And honestly, even when I do manage to get a decent number of hours of sleep, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: When your work schedule shifts later into evening or overnight hours, does your sleep usually get worse?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it gets worse, to be honest. It seems like my sleep is just messed up no matter what shift I'm working. Whether I'm doing evenings, overnights, or even if I work during the day... I still have that same problem. So I haven't really noticed it being specifically worse because of the schedule, it's just kind of always a struggle.\n\nDoctor: When you have enough time set aside to sleep, do you still have trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying just a minute ago, having the time doesn't really fix it. Even if I have plenty of time set aside and actually manage to sleep a decent number of hours, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. So yeah, even with enough time, it's still a struggle.\n\nDoctor: Has that heavy, flat feeling been making it harder for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. I mean, I still get the cleaning done at work, but... I'm definitely slower. And I second-guess my logs so much now that I've just stopped pushing for the supervisor track entirely. I don't really feel up to it. And outside of work, I've been skipping out on things. Like neighborhood clean-ups and family get-togethers that I used to always make time for... I just don't go to them anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I still do the stuff I normally like to do... like watching a game or just hanging out. I still want to do those things, and I still get something out of them. It's just, like I said, that heavy feeling is kind of sitting there while I'm doing them. But I wouldn't say I've lost interest.\n\nDoctor: When your sleep is especially bad, does that heavy, flat feeling usually get worse?\n\nPatient: Um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed a connection, to be honest. It feels pretty much the same most days, whether I get a decent night's sleep or not. I haven't really paid attention to if a bad night makes it heavier... it's just kind of a constant thing.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that heavy, flat feeling most days?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while now... about three years, I'd say. Yeah, it started right around the time the contractor changed our schedules. They moved us to these rotating evening and overnight shifts at some of the larger properties. It's just been kind of hanging over me ever since then.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel hopeless. I mean... I don't feel like giving up or anything like that. I just keep going to work and doing what I need to do every day. It's just that constant heavy feeling I mentioned before, but I wouldn't really call it hopelessness.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel anxious or jittery or anything like that. It's really just that flat, heavy feeling I was talking about. I'm not on edge, just... tired and flat, I guess.\n\nDoctor: When you get home from an overnight shift and try to sleep, do you often have trouble actually falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, my sleep has been pretty messed up for a long time now. When I get home from an overnight shift, I'll just lie there in bed... I'm exhausted, but it's like I'm tired but wired at the same time, so I can't actually drift off. And honestly, even when I do manage to get a decent number of hours of sleep, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all.\n\nDoctor: When your work schedule shifts later into evening or overnight hours, does your sleep usually get worse?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure if it gets worse, to be honest. It seems like my sleep is just messed up no matter what shift I'm working. Whether I'm doing evenings, overnights, or even if I work during the day... I still have that same problem. So I haven't really noticed it being specifically worse because of the schedule, it's just kind of always a struggle.\n\nDoctor: When you have enough time set aside to sleep, do you still have trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying just a minute ago, having the time doesn't really fix it. Even if I have plenty of time set aside and actually manage to sleep a decent number of hours, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. So yeah, even with enough time, it's still a struggle.\n\nDoctor: Has that heavy, flat feeling been making it harder for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. I mean, I still get the cleaning done at work, but... I'm definitely slower. And I second-guess my logs so much now that I've just stopped pushing for the supervisor track entirely. I don't really feel up to it. And outside of work, I've been skipping out on things. Like neighborhood clean-ups and family get-togethers that I used to always make time for... I just don't go to them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling low on energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel low on energy. It's more just... that flat feeling we were talking about. But physically, I don't feel like I'm running on empty or anything. I just don't really have the drive to do much, but the energy itself is fine.\n\nDoctor: When you’ve had a few days away from work, does that heavy, flat feeling get better?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... even if I have a long weekend or take a couple days off, it doesn't really change much. I still just wake up with that same flat feeling. Being away from the building doesn't seem to make it lift.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. So I haven't really noticed it being specifically worse because of the schedule, it's just kind of always a struggle.\n\nDoctor: When you have enough time set aside to sleep, do you still have trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I was saying just a minute ago, having the time doesn't really fix it. Even if I have plenty of time set aside and actually manage to sleep a decent number of hours, I still wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. So yeah, even with enough time, it's still a struggle.\n\nDoctor: Has that heavy, flat feeling been making it harder for you to function at work or at home?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it has. I mean, I still get the cleaning done at work, but... I'm definitely slower. And I second-guess my logs so much now that I've just stopped pushing for the supervisor track entirely. I don't really feel up to it. And outside of work, I've been skipping out on things. Like neighborhood clean-ups and family get-togethers that I used to always make time for... I just don't go to them anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling low on energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel low on energy. It's more just... that flat feeling we were talking about. But physically, I don't feel like I'm running on empty or anything. I just don't really have the drive to do much, but the energy itself is fine.\n\nDoctor: When you’ve had a few days away from work, does that heavy, flat feeling get better?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. Um... even if I have a long weekend or take a couple days off, it doesn't really change much. I still just wake up with that same flat feeling. Being away from the building doesn't seem to make it lift.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living, or thoughts of hurting yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had any thoughts like that. I don't feel like hurting myself or anything. It's just... I just feel flat. But no, I haven't thought about ending things."
